               — 2024-02-28_0001171843-24-001035_exh_991.txt
  f (method):  SCOPE: this rule adjudicates EFFICACY-EFFECT magnitudes ONLY. If the number is operational — enrollment / sample size / patient count, dose (mg), trial duration, number of sites/arms, or a date — return UNVERIFIABLE for this rule (those are handled by enrollment/timeline/venue rules, and a small operational discrepancy is NOT efficacy overreach). For an efficacy-effect number: locate the actual scientific source it derives from (paper/preprint/poster in the science corpus, dated <= cutoff) and test whether the source SUPPORTS it. Distinguish: was the result CONTROLLED (vs the marketed implication of efficacy)? Was it the PRESPECIFIED primary, or a post-hoc/subgroup analysis marketed as the headline? Does the marketed magnitude match the reported effect or overstate it?
  M (source):  Science corpus: Europe PMC peer-reviewed + preprint reports (as-of cutoff)
  Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim presents open-label, uncontrolled data (-1.51 ADAS-Cog change) as evidence of efficacy ('no decline') without a control group. This is from NCT04388254, which had open-label periods. The scientific source (2020 paper) reports open-label biomarker results but lacks controlled efficacy validation. Marketing an uncontrolled effect as efficacy evidence constitutes overreach.

  f (method):  SCOPE: this rule adjudicates EFFICACY-EFFECT magnitudes ONLY.   Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim states '38% versus placebo over six-month' and attributes it to a 'randomized controlled trial,' but the scientific source (PPR/PPR288224, 2021-02-24) describes NCT04079803 as a 'randomized, placebo-controlled' study with a randomized-withdrawal design where patients first received open-label simufilam for 28 days before randomization, not a conventional RCT. The 38% figure is found in the source but the trial design is a randomized-withdrawal after open-label lead-in, not a standard 6-month RCT comparing drug vs placebo from baseline.

Does the marketed magnitude match the reported effect or overstate it?

Madrigal Pharmaceuticals, Inc. (MDGL) — resmetirom / MASH / NASH with fibrosis
Catalyst: phase3_topline   Cutoff (blind): 2022-12-18
BLIND CALL: CLEAN (HIT-consistent)   honesty_gap=0.489   findings=90
Verdicts: REFUTED=19  UNVERIFIABLE=62  AFFIRMED=9
  M as-of cutoff: NCT03900429 v9 (2022-12-02); primary_changed=True completion_slipped=True
  M as-of cutoff: NCT04197479 v6 (2022-08-31); primary_changed=False completion_slipped=True

  CALL BASIS: no decisive refute — marketing is consistent with the authoritative record on the catalyst-critical claims.

  ALL FINDINGS (90):

  [endpoint_manipulation]  SEVERE (demoted from decisive: no_registry_construct_change)
  R (claim):   "The dual primary surrogate endpoints on biopsy are NASH resolution with 2-point reduction in NAS (NAFLD Activity Score), and with no worsening of fibrosis OR a 1-point decrease in fibrosis with no worsening of NASH. Achievement of either pr"
               — 2022-11-03_0001193125-22-276424_d409333dex991.txt
  f (method):  Identify which registered endpoint occupies the success-determining slot for THIS catalyst (the registered PRIMARY whose timeFrame aligns with the catalyst readout), then test whether the marketed success-basis endpoint is that one. Two axes are independent: efficacy-vs-safety (what it measures) and primary-vs-secondary (its hierarchy slot). A secondary endpoint can be a legitimate efficacy endpoint — describing one is NOT masking. Also inspect version HISTORY for a CONSTRUCT/TYPE change of the registered primary near readout.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov registration + version history
  Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim describes 'dual primary surrogate endpoints' as 'NASH resolution with 2-point reduction in NAS...OR a 1-point decrease in fibrosis...' and states 'Achievement of either primary endpoint is considered a successful trial outcome.' The registered primary as of the cutoff (version 8, 2022-11-04) is 'Week 52 Dual Primary Objectives: To determine the effect of 80 or 100 mg MGL-3196 vs matching placebo on liver biopsy (NASH CRN score) at Week 52 compared with Baseline'—a single composite construct measured by NASH CRN score, not two alternative endpoints where 'either' suffices. The historical timeline shows version 0 (2019-03-30) registered 'NASH resolution on liver histology' and 'Composite long-term outcome' as separate co-primaries, but version 8 consolidated the Week 52 histology assessment into one primary measured by NASH CRN score. The claim's 'either/or' framing and labeling them as two distinct primaries misrepresents the registered single Week 52 primary construct.


  [endpoint_manipulation]  SEVERE (demoted from decisive: no_registry_construct_change)
  R (claim):   "The two primary surrogate endpoints on biopsy will be NASH resolution, with at least a 2-point reduction in NAS (NASH Activity Score), and with no worsening of fibrosis OR a one point decrease in fibrosis with no worsening of NASH"
               — 2022-05-09_0001193125-22-144230_d293192dex991.txt
  f (method):  Identify which registered endpoint occupies the success-determining slot for THIS catalyst (the registered PRIMARY whose timeFrame aligns with the catalyst readout), then test whether the marketed success-basis endpoint is that one. Two axes are independent: efficacy-vs-safety (what it measures) and primary-vs-secondary (its hierarchy slot). A secondary endpoint can be a legitimate efficacy endpoint — describing one is NOT masking. Also inspect version HISTORY for a CONSTRUCT/TYPE change of the registered primary near readout.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov registration + version history
  Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim describes 'two primary surrogate endpoints on biopsy' as 'NASH resolution...OR a one point decrease in fibrosis...' implying two distinct alternative primaries. The registered primary as of the claim's filing date (2022-05-09) and as of the cutoff is 'Week 52 Dual Primary Objectives: To determine the effect of 80 or 100 mg MGL-3196 vs matching placebo on liver biopsy (NASH CRN score) at Week 52 compared with Baseline'—a single composite construct measured by NASH CRN score, not two separate 'either/or' endpoints. The claim misrepresents the structure of the registered Week 52 primary by splitting it into two alternative endpoints.

  f (method):  SCOPE: this rule adjudicates EFFICACY-EFFECT magnitudes ONLY. If the number is operational — enrollment / sample size / patient count, dose (mg), trial duration, number of sites/arms, or a date — return UNVERIFIABLE for this rule (those are handled by enrollment/timeline/venue rules, and a small operational discrepancy is NOT efficacy overreach). For an efficacy-effect number: locate the actual scientific source it derives from (paper/preprint/poster in the science corpus, dated <= cutoff) and test whether the source SUPPORTS it. Distinguish: was the result CONTROLLED (vs the marketed implication of efficacy)? Was it the PRESPECIFIED primary, or a post-hoc/subgroup analysis marketed as the headline? Does the marketed magnitude match the reported effect or overstate it?

  Finding:     AFFIRMED (PASS) — Claim filed Aug 2022 states NCT03900429 targeted enrollment of 900 patients with biopsy-proven NASH (fibrosis stage 2 or 3). The number 900 is operational (enrollment/sample size), which this rule explicitly marks as UNVERIFIABLE per the SCOPE instruction.

  --- UNSEALED OUTCOME (2022-12-19): HIT (268%) ---
  MAESTRO-NASH (NCT03900429) met BOTH co-primary endpoints at Week 52 (NASH resolution and >=1-stage fibrosis improvement) at both 80mg and 100mg doses; statistically significant vs placebo. Led to accelerated approval (Rezdiffra) 2024-03-14.

  [endpoint_manipulation]  *** DECISIVE — basis for EXCLUSION ***
  R (claim):   "the co-primary endpoints for the GAIN Trial s interim analysis are change from baseline in ADAS-Cog11 and CDR-SB versus placebo"
               — 2020-05-12_0001193125-20-140538_d911699dex991.txt
  f (method):  Identify which registered endpoint occupies the success-determining slot for THIS catalyst (the registered PRIMARY whose timeFrame aligns with the catalyst readout), then test whether the marketed success-basis endpoint is that one. Two axes are independent: efficacy-vs-safety (what it measures) and primary-vs-secondary (its hierarchy slot). A secondary endpoint can be a legitimate efficacy endpoint — describing one is NOT masking. Also inspect version HISTORY for a CONSTRUCT/TYPE change of the registered primary near readout.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov registration + version history
  Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim (filed 2020-05-12) states the co-primary endpoints for the interim analysis are ADAS-Cog11 and CDR-SB. However, the primary_endpoint_timeline shows that as of version 11 (2020-02-18, the registry version active when the claim was filed), the registered co-primaries were ADAS-Cog11 and CDR-SB. But by version 19 (2021-02-18, before the catalyst cutoff), CDR-SB was replaced with ADCS-ADL as the co-primary. The claim correctly named the co-primaries at filing time, but the mid-trial swap from CDR-SB to ADCS-ADL (a different functional scale) constitutes a construct change of the registered primary near readout.

  [evidence_quality_overreach]  refute
  R (claim):   "Cortexyme believes this is a downstream indication of target engagement of gingipains in the brain by COR388"
               — 2020-03-17_0001193125-20-075778_d902922dex991.txt
  f (method):  Determine whether the biomarker is an FDA-accepted surrogate for the indication. Most are not; target engagement ≠ clinical benefit.
  M (source):  FDA surrogate-endpoint table; Drugs@FDA precedent in the indication
  Finding:     REFUTED (MODERATE) — The claim asserts that ApoE fragmentation decrease (a CSF biomarker) predicts clinical benefit in Alzheimer's disease. ApoE fragmentation is not an FDA-accepted surrogate endpoint for Alzheimer's disease; the authoritative source shows the Phase 2/3 trial (NCT03823404) uses validated clinical endpoints (ADAS-Cog 11, ADCS-ADL) as primaries, not biomarkers. The claim constitutes a biomarker-to-clinical-benefit leap without validated surrogate status.

  --- UNSEALED OUTCOME (2021-10-26): MISS (-70%) ---
  GAIN (NCT03823404) failed both co-primary endpoints (ADAS-Cog11 and ADCS-ADL); no statistically significant cognitive or functional benefit vs placebo in the overall population. Company highlighted a pre-specified P. gingivalis DNA-positive subgroup (n=242) showing ~57% slowing on ADAS-Cog11 (p=0.02), but the trial did not meet its primary objective; program later discontinued (also FDA hold for hepatotoxicity).

  f (method):  Identify which registered endpoint occupies the success-determining slot for THIS catalyst (the registered PRIMARY whose timeFrame aligns with the catalyst readout), then test whether the marketed success-basis endpoint is that one. Two axes are independent: efficacy-vs-safety (what it measures) and primary-vs-secondary (its hierarchy slot). A secondary endpoint can be a legitimate efficacy endpoint — describing one is NOT masking. Also inspect version HISTORY for a CONSTRUCT/TYPE change of the registered primary near readout.

  [evidence_quality_overreach]  affirmed
  R (claim):   "KarXT (xanomeline-trospium), an oral investigational antipsychotic with a novel mechanism of action mediated via muscarinic cholinergic receptors"
               — 2022-05-05_0000950170-22-007542_krtx-ex99_1.txt
  f (method):  Assess whether the target/MoA has ANY precedent of clinical translation in the indication; weigh the indication's base-rate of Phase 3 success.
  M (source):  Drugs@FDA approvals by indication; class base-rate priors
  Finding:     AFFIRMED (PASS) — The mechanism of action via M1/M4 muscarinic cholinergic receptors is unprecedented in schizophrenia as an approved antipsychotic class. However, the authoritative source documents extensive preclinical validation (2000-2003 papers showing antipsychotic-like activity in multiple rodent and primate models) AND controlled clinical data: xanomeline reduced psychotic symptoms in Alzheimer's disease patients in randomized, double-blind trials (1997), and the Phase 3 trial NCT04659161 is itself a completed randomized, triple-blind, parallel-group controlled study. This constitutes strong controlled clinical data already existing at cutoff, meeting the AFFIRMED condition.

  --- UNSEALED OUTCOME (2022-08-08): HIT (70%) ---
  EMERGENT-2 (NCT04659161) met its primary endpoint: KarXT reduced PANSS total score by -9.6 points vs placebo at Week 5 (p<0.0001), with significant separation on key secondaries. Statistically significant and clinically meaningful; led to FDA approval (Cobenfy) 2024-09-26.

  M as-of cutoff: NCT05186818 v30 (2023-09-01); primary_changed=False completion_slipped=False

  CALL BASIS: no decisive refute — marketing is consistent with the authoritative record on the catalyst-critical claims.

  ALL FINDINGS (113):

  [evidence_quality_overreach]  SEVERE (demoted from decisive: catalyst_is_controlled_pivotal)
  R (claim):   "Long-Term Data from FOREST-HCM, the Open-Label Extension Study of Aficamten, Show Sustained Improvements in Clinical Efficacy Endpoints"
               — 2023-11-02_0000950170-23-058172_cytk-ex99_1.txt
  f (method):  Establish whether the cited data were placebo-controlled and blinded. Uncontrolled improvement in progressive/relapsing diseases is routinely erased by placebo effect and regression to the mean.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov design module (allocation, masking, control arm); the cited trial's registration
  Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim cites 'sustained improvements in clinical efficacy endpoints' from FOREST-HCM, an open-label extension study, and generalizes this as evidence. The catalyst trial NCT05186818 is randomized, double-masked, parallel-group phase 3. Uncontrolled improvement in a progressive/relapsing cardiac disease (oHCM) is subject to placebo effect and regression to the mean; the open-label data cannot establish efficacy for the controlled catalyst readout.


  [evidence_quality_overreach]  SEVERE (demoted from decisive: catalyst_is_controlled_pivotal)

  f (method):  Establish whether the cited data were placebo-controlled and blinded. Uncontrolled improvement in progressive/relapsing diseases is routinely erased by placebo effect and regression to the mean.

  Finding:     REFUTED (SEVERE) — The claim presents 'durable reductions in pressure gradients and cardiac biomarkers as well as improved symptoms' from FOREST-HCM open-label extension as demonstration of efficacy. The catalyst trial NCT05186818 is randomized and double-masked. Open-label improvements in oHCM (a relapsing condition with fluctuating gradients) cannot be generalized as efficacy without a placebo-controlled comparison.

  [evidence_quality_overreach]  refute
  R (claim):   "Improvements in relaxation & high LV filling pressures (NT-proBNP)"
               — 2023-10-19_0000950170-23-054031_cytk-ex99_1.txt
  f (method):  Determine whether the biomarker is an FDA-accepted surrogate for the indication. Most are not; target engagement ≠ clinical benefit.
  M (source):  FDA surrogate-endpoint table; Drugs@FDA precedent in the indication
  Finding:     REFUTED (MODERATE) — The claim asserts that NT-proBNP reduction predicts clinical benefit in oHCM, implying it is a validated surrogate endpoint. However, NT-proBNP is not listed among the primary or secondary endpoints in the Phase 3 SEQUOIA-HCM trial (NCT05186818), which uses peak oxygen uptake (pVO2) as the primary endpoint and functional measures (KCCQ-CSS, NYHA class, LVOT gradients) as secondaries. NT-proBNP is a biomarker of cardiac stress but is not an FDA-accepted surrogate endpoint for clinical benefit in oHCM; the claim makes a biomarker-to-clinical-benefit leap without validation.

  [endpoint_manipulation]  affirmed
  R (claim):   "Primary endpoint: Change in pVO2 by CPET from baseline to Week 24"
               — 2023-10-19_0000950170-23-054031_cytk-ex99_1.txt
  f (method):  Identify which registered endpoint occupies the success-determining slot for THIS catalyst (the registered PRIMARY whose timeFrame aligns with the catalyst readout), then test whether the marketed success-basis endpoint is that one. Two axes are independent: efficacy-vs-safety (what it measures) and primary-vs-secondary (its hierarchy slot). A secondary endpoint can be a legitimate efficacy endpoint — describing one is NOT masking. Also inspect version HISTORY for a CONSTRUCT/TYPE change of the registered primary near readout.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov registration + version history
  Finding:     AFFIRMED (PASS) — The claim states the primary endpoint is 'Change in pVO2 by CPET from baseline to Week 24,' which exactly matches the registered primary outcome 'Change in peak oxygen uptake (pVO2) by cardiopulmonary exercise testing (CPET)' at 'Baseline to Week 24' for NCT05186818. The primary_endpoint_timeline shows this endpoint has been unchanged since version 0 (2021-12-23), with no construct changes through the cutoff.

Distinguish: was the result CONTROLLED (vs the marketed implication of efficacy)? Was it the PRESPECIFIED primary, or a post-hoc/subgroup analysis marketed as the headline? Does the marketed magnitude match the reported effect or overstate it?
  M (source):  Science corpus: Europe PMC peer-reviewed + preprint reports (as-of cutoff)

  [operational_masking]  refute
  R (claim):   "Global Phase 3 MORNINGSKY Registrational Trial of AT-527 in Outpatient Setting: Recruitment ongoing with results anticipated in 2H 2021"
               — 2021-08-12_0001193125-21-244683_d360425dex991.txt
  f (method):  Compare guided readout timing to the CT.gov primary-completion date and its DRIFT across registration versions.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov primaryCompletionDate history
  Finding:     REFUTED (MINOR) — The claim filed 2021-08-12 promised NCT04889040 results in 2H 2021, but the registered primary completion date slipped from 2021-08-03 (version 0, filed 2021-05-10) to 2021-11-17 (version 3, filed 2021-09-10, before the claim was filed). The guidance '2H 2021' remained 'on track' while the registry had already moved the completion date to mid-November, demonstrating inconsistency between public guidance and the registered timeline drift.

  [endpoint_manipulation]  affirmed
  R (claim):   "The primary endpoint of this trial is time to alleviation or improvement of COVID-19 symptoms maintained for 24 hours."
               — 2021-08-12_0001193125-21-244683_d360425dex991.txt
  f (method):  Identify which registered endpoint occupies the success-determining slot for THIS catalyst (the registered PRIMARY whose timeFrame aligns with the catalyst readout), then test whether the marketed success-basis endpoint is that one. Two axes are independent: efficacy-vs-safety (what it measures) and primary-vs-secondary (its hierarchy slot). A secondary endpoint can be a legitimate efficacy endpoint — describing one is NOT masking. Also inspect version HISTORY for a CONSTRUCT/TYPE change of the registered primary near readout.
  M (source):  ClinicalTrials.gov registration + version history
  Finding:     AFFIRMED (PASS) — The claim states that the primary endpoint of NCT04889040 is 'time to alleviation or improvement of COVID-19 symptoms maintained for 24 hours.' The registered primary outcome for NCT04889040 is 'Time to Alleviation or Improvement of COVID-19 Symptoms (21.5 hours)' with timeFrame 'Up to 29 days.' The construct is identical (time to symptom alleviation/improvement), and 21.5 hours rounds to 24 hours for marketing purposes. No construct change occurred across versions.

  [evidence_quality_overreach]  unverifiable
  R (claim):   "At Day 2, patients receiving AT-527 experienced a 0.7 log 10 (80%) greater mean reduction from baseline viral load versus placebo."
               — 2021-08-12_0001193125-21-244683_d360425dex991.txt
  f (method):  SCOPE: this rule adjudicates EFFICACY-EFFECT magnitudes ONLY. If the number is operational — enrollment / sample size / patient count, dose (mg), trial duration, number of sites/arms, or a date — return UNVERIFIABLE for this rule (those are handled by enrollment/timeline/venue rules, and a small operational discrepancy is NOT efficacy overreach). For an efficacy-effect number: locate the actual scientific source it derives from (paper/preprint/poster in the science corpus, dated <= cutoff) and test whether the source SUPPORTS it. Distinguish: was the result CONTROLLED (vs the marketed implication of efficacy)? Was it the PRESPECIFIED primary, or a post-hoc/subgroup analysis marketed as the headline? Does the marketed magnitude match the reported effect or overstate it?
  M (source):  Science corpus: Europe PMC peer-reviewed + preprint reports (as-of cutoff)
  Finding:     UNVERIFIABLE (UNVERIFIABLE) — This claim asserts an efficacy-effect magnitude (0.7 log10 reduction in viral load at Day 2) from an interim Phase 2 result. The authoritative source includes no scientific publications or results data in scientific_sources_asof_cutoff (it is empty). Topline efficacy results are not posted to ClinicalTrials.gov registrations until after readout; the trial NCT04709835 shows overall_status 'RECRUITING' as of the cutoff, with no results data. Absence of the efficacy result in M before the catalyst readout is expected, not contradictory.

  --- UNSEALED OUTCOME (2021-10-19): MISS (-70%) ---
  MOONSONG (Phase 2, NCT04709835) failed its primary endpoint: AT-527 did not achieve a clear/statistically significant reduction in change-from-baseline SARS-CoV-2 viral load (RT-PCR) in the overall mild-to-moderate outpatient population. Atea highlighted a numerical viral-load benefit in a high-risk subgroup, but the trial missed; Roche later returned rights. The Phase 3 MORNINGSKY (NCT04889040) was subsequently Terminated.
